# run9 英語レビュー: ワールド27・28（レッスン 781〜840）

- 日付: 2026-11-27
- やりかた: docs/run9/BRIEF.md の「Review method」。lint → 3人の レビュアー（A=アメリカの 小学校の 先生 / B=こどもの ESL・言語習得の 専門家 / C=台本を 読む 声優）を べつべつに 書いて から 決める。2人 いじょうが 指摘 or 客観的な まちがい（objective）だけ なおす。10 レッスンずつ 6 グループで レビュー
- lint（`npm run english:lint -- --from 781 --to 840`）: **まえ error 0 / warn 60 / info 8 → あと error 0 / warn 32 / info 8**（まえの warn: autumn・football の uk-word 23・okay 4・「…」と おわりの 記号 32・stiff「What's your hobby」1。あとの のこりは ぜんぶ わざと: 会話・スライドの「…」〈ためらい・ま〉と タイトル「I Think… Because…」〈テストで きまって いる〉、てがみの むすび「Your friend, Kiko」「― Your friend, Haru」、L795 しゅみの レッスンの「What's your hobby?」）
- なおした 欄: **446**（パッチ 472 件。機械的な なおし 6・ブロックの きめごと 154・AI の さいしょの ひとこと 56・レビュアー 228・あとの そろえ 28）。なおした レッスン: **60 / 60**
- 60 レッスン ぜんぶに `"reviewed": "run9"` を つけた
- パッチは ぜんぶ「before が いまの 値と おなじ ときだけ あてる」で、HEAD から じゅんばんに あてなおした（はずれ 0）。60 レッスン ぜんぶで スライドの 検証（unknownSlideItems）・台本の 長さ（180〜240 秒）・英語の わりあいを たしかめた（ALL OK）。かいわ あいての チェック（.run9-w2728/partner.ts）も ALL OK

## 共通の きめごと（ブロック全体）
1. 話しことばの「Say, A. Or, B.」→「Or b.」（L781・L782・L786 と L786 の teacherScript）
2. 「okay」→「OK」（L823・L833）、「autumn / Autumn」→「fall / Fall」（L818・L821・L838）、「football」→「soccer」（L837。日本語は もともと サッカー）、「pen friend」→「pen pal」・「ペンフレンド」→「ペンパル」（L808。L826 と W21 は もともと pen pal）
3. イギリスふうの ほめことば: 「Brilliant!」→「Fantastic! / Great job!」（L788・L797・L802・L814）、「Lovely work!」→「Great work!」（L818・L836。グループで L785・L804 の「Lovely!」も）
4. エマ先生 じぶんの 語りの「Ms. Emma says / asks」→「I say / I ask」、日本語「エマ先生「」→「わたし「」（18 レッスン。グループで「Ms. Emma smiles / gives / takes / tells」なども「I ～」に）
5. エマ先生の あいさつの「Good morning / Good evening, everyone!」→「Hi / Hello, everyone!」・日本語「やあ / こんにちは」（L782・L786・L787・L791・L794・L797・L806・L813・L818・L824。ものがたりの じかんは つぎの 文に のこる）
6. 「A person who / A small thing that / All the people who / someone who」→ ふつうの 文（L820 guide・L828 clue・L834 audience・L810「Think of a helper in your life.」。関係代名詞は ステージ4）
7. **AI の さいしょの ひとこと**: アプリは あいてを こどもの キャラ（ハル・リコ・キコ・ビリモ）から えらび、その かおと なまえで ひとことを 出す。なのに「I'm Granny Capy.」「It's Toto.」「Moi!（アイノ として）」など べつの 人で 話して いた（56 レッスン）。goalJa が やくを きめて いるので やくは のこし、「Let's pretend I'm Granny Capy / Dr. Fern / Toto / Iris / Aino / Amara / Mateo / Leo / Ms. Emma / your pen pal / Sleepy Squirrel.」で ごっこあそびだと わかる ように した（W25-26 と おなじ）。あさの あいさつ「Good morning」は「Hi / Hello」に、2つめの Let's は We can に。L783・L785・L790 は ひとことが だれでも いえる ので、goalJa の「AI の エマ先生」を ほんとうの あいて（キコ・キコ・リコ）に した
8. あとの そろえ（グループの 報告から。20-after.json）:
   - ch = k の いいかたを「The C H makes a k sound.」に そろえた（L795・L800・L806・L808・L810。たんどくの「k」は TTS が「ケイ」と よむ かも しれない。L781〜L785 は グループが おなじ かたちに）
   - karaoke の 英語の よみを「carry-OH-key」に そろえた（L818・L840。2つの グループが べつの つづりに して いた。日本語の ヒント「キャリオウキ」と あう）
   - つながる 音の ふりかえり（L835 meet-chu・L836 could-joo・L839 aw-low-ver）を もとの レッスン（L826・L829）の つづりに そろえた。「Aw-lover」は lover と よまれるので グループが なおした
   - scripts/english-words.txt の 音の つづりに「ver」を たした（aw-low-ver。先生の 台本・字幕だけで ゆるす）

## 学習項目 ID が かわった もの（記録が あたらしく なる）
- L808 ことば「pen friend」→「pen pal」
- L818 表現「When is the best time to visit? ― I think autumn is the best. The leaves turn red.」→「… I think fall is the best. …」（aiTask の targetExpressions・まとめ・スライドも あわせた）
- テスト・src の 変更は なし（テストは どちらの 文字も みて いない）

## のこした こと（1人だけの 指摘・テストで きまって いる・あとで 決める）
- 会話と スライドの「…」、タイトル「I Think… Because…」、てがみの むすびの おわりの 記号なし、L795「What's your hobby?」（しゅみの レッスン）
- L815 core「In both countries, people celebrate New Year.」（アメリカでは New Year's が ふつう だが テストで きまって いる）、L827 core「I've made this for you.」（アメリカでは I made が ふつう だが テストで きまって いる）、L792 core「What have you done for a long time?」（テスト）
- L793 ことば「foot spring」（アメリカなら foot bath。学習項目で 8か所 あり、1グループだけの 指摘）、L801 TPR「Hold your hands.」、L810「half past ten」
- 「VI-ta-min」（L811 ほか。フォニックスの sounds「VI」と あわせて いる）、「Pi-AN-o」「SU-shi」などの 大文字の 音節、たんどくの「Bow!」（L811・L819）。TTS で きいて たしかめたい もの
- L792「Riko, that was lovely!」・L781「What a lovely village!」（キャラの ことば。アメリカでも つかう）、L784 ファーン先生の「Tell Ms. Emma if you feel worse.」（ほかの 人の ことば）、L796・L802・L832・L834 の goalJa の「エマ先生（AI）」（ひとことが「Let's pretend I'm Ms. Emma.」なので あう）
- ゲストの あいさつ「Moi! / Jambo! / Hola!」（ほんとうの ことば）

## レビュアーの メモ（グループごと・英語）

### Run 9 review notes: L781-790 (world 27, Stage 3)

These notes cover school expressions and grammar, vocab, TPR, phonics words and tips, conversation lines and aiTask, recap, teacherScript, and every teacher segment (say, subtitleJa, subtitleEn, slide), plus testPoints. I did not redo or undo the block-wide fixes (Or b. running examples, OK, US praise, I say / I ask, Hi / Hello openings, "Let's pretend I'm" starters). The L781/L782/L786 seg1/seg17 subtitleEn already match their say lines in the current dump, so no patch was needed there. There are 42 patches in 9 lessons; L786 has no change. There are no learning-item ID changes and no changes to school.expressions[].en, vocab[].en, tpr[].en or phonics words. The health language is kind and safe (rest, water, tell a grown-up; nine to twelve hours of sleep in L787/L788; no medicine), and the only present perfect before L791 is the L784 "How long have you had a fever? ― Since yesterday." frame (plus "Haru has had a fever since yesterday" in the L784 teacherScript, allowed). "How often" first appears in L786. Themes: (1) **TTS and phonics wording**: "C H says k" / "it says k" (L781-785) makes TTS say the letter name "kay" as if it were the sound; changed to "makes a k sound", which is how US teachers name it. L781 also had a bare "says ch". L785 split syllables with commas ("Stom, ach, ache"); changed to the hyphen beat form. (2) **Ms. Emma in the third person**: the block fix covered "Ms. Emma says / asks", but "Ms. Emma tells" (L783), "Ms. Emma takes him" and "Let's go with Ms. Emma" (L784) and "Ms. Emma smiles" (L789) were still there. (3) **goalJa names Ms. Emma as the AI partner** (L783, L785, L790), but the app never picks Ms. Emma; I changed it to the real partner child. (4) **Small wording**: L782 point segment made the same child say both "I have a headache" and "That's too bad"; "Lots of music together" for orchestra (L783); "Thank you, doctor." (L784); "Lovely!" (L785); "a folded paper" (L785); "Good night, and see you!" (L787); "Tell good habits" (L788). Every new sentence has 15 words or fewer. The check script passes with ALL OK. All changed lessons are 209-226 s long with 100% English, and every slide item is valid.

#### L781 The Village of Springs
- A: The arrival, Granny Capy and the Spring Inn sign are natural and warm. "Please do not swim in the springs" is fine on a sign. Ms. Emma's "What a lovely village!" is normal US speech (not praise of work; kept). seg8 "Then tell how it feels" could be "say how it feels" (single flag, kept).
- B: The "The ~ here is warm" frame and "Can we ~?" are clear. The ch = /k/ tip is correct. seg9 "C H usually says ch, like cheese. But sometimes it says k!" names sounds with bare letters.
- C: Bare "ch" may be read as "see aitch" or a stray syllable; "it says k" / "Hear the k?" will be read "kay". The teacherScript "Today, c h says k." has the same problem.
- Decision: teacherScript "c h says k" -> "c h makes a k sound" (B+C). seg9 (say, subtitleEn) -> "C H usually sounds like the start of cheese. But sometimes it makes a k sound! Echo. School. Hear the k sound?" with JA cheese の はじめの おと / 「ク」の おとに なるよ (B+C).

#### L782 What's Wrong?
- A: Kiko's headache after the trip, the herbal tea and Granny Capy's card are natural; the card ends with "please tell a grown-up". Kiko repeats "I'm tired" in line 4 after line 1, but it is the core expression (kept).
- B: seg8 "Answer with I have a, and then say, That's too bad." tells the same child to answer and then sympathize; the JA correctly says きいた ひとは. EN/JA mismatch. The -ache tip (silent e, ch = k) is correct.
- C: "Answer with I have a, and then" hangs on "a" (TTS reads "uh" or "ay"). seg9 "The C H says k" has the letter-name problem.
- Decision: seg8 (say, subtitleEn) -> "Answer, I have a headache. Then your friend says, That's too bad." with JA to match (B+C). seg9 (say, subtitleEn) "says k" -> "makes a k sound" (B+C).

#### L783 My Stomach Hurts
- A: The village fair, Birimo eating too much and the food stall menu are fun and natural ("Your stomach will thank you!"). seg10 "Orchestra. Lots of music together." is not what an orchestra is (a group of musicians). The teacherScript says "Ms. Emma tells Birimo to sit and rest." in Ms. Emma's own voice.
- B: Same flags. The JA already says たくさんの がっきで いっしょに えんそう. goalJa says AI の エマ先生, but the app picks Kiko (wrap-up "talk with Kiko"); the starter works for Kiko as is. The stomach / bellyache / orchestra ch = k list is correct.
- C: "The C H says k" (seg9, seg10, teacherScript) has the letter-name problem.
- Decision: teacherScript "Ms. Emma tells" -> "I tell" and the two "c h says k" -> "makes a k sound" (A+B). goalJa AI の エマ先生と -> AI の キコと (objective JA). seg9 (say, subtitleEn) "says k" -> "makes a k sound" (B+C). seg10 (say, subtitleEn) -> "Orchestra. Many musicians play together. C H makes a k sound here, too." (A+B).

#### L784 At the Clinic
- A: Dr. Fern's clinic, "Open your mouth and say ah", "Cough into your elbow" and the note are real and safe (rest, water, tell an adult). conversation line 5 "Thank you, doctor." uses lowercase doctor in direct address. The teacherScript "Ms. Emma takes him to the little clinic" and seg0 "Let's go with Ms. Emma to the little clinic" have Ms. Emma talking about herself.
- B: The present perfect is limited to "How long have you had a fever? ― Since yesterday." (allowed in L784), with the fever（ねつ） hint. "Since means the start" plus the slide is clear. The Christmas silent t is correct.
- C: "c h says k" (teacherScript, seg9, seg10) has the letter-name problem. "Ahhh" is fine.
- Decision: teacherScript "Ms. Emma takes him" -> "I take him" and two "c h says k" -> "makes a k sound" (A+B). seg0 (say, subtitleEn) -> "Let's take him to the little clinic." with JA ハルくんを … つれて いこう (A+B). seg9, seg10 (say, subtitleEn) "says k" -> "makes a k sound" (B+C). conversation.lines[5].en "Thank you, doctor." -> "Thank you, Dr. Fern." (objective; JA 先生 still fits).

#### L785 I Hope You Feel Better
- A: The get-well card is sweet and natural ("We miss you! The village is quiet without you."). The teacherScript praise "Lovely!" is British (the block changed Lovely / Brilliant elsewhere). seg3 "A folded paper" should be "a folded piece of paper".
- B: "I hope + good thing" is well explained. goalJa says AI の エマ先生 but the partner is Kiko. seg10 "Head, ache. Stom, ach, ache." splits syllables with commas, which looks like separate words.
- C: "Stom, ach" may be read with a German "ach"; the brief asks for the hyphen beat form. "C H can say k" / "C h says k" has the letter-name problem.
- Decision: teacherScript "Lovely!" -> "Great!" and "C h says k (again)" -> "C h makes a k sound (again)" (A+B). goalJa AI の エマ先生と -> AI の キコと (objective JA). seg3 (say, subtitleEn) "A folded paper" -> "A folded piece of paper" (A+B). seg9 (say, subtitleEn) "C H can say k" -> "C H can make a k sound" (B+C). seg10 (say, subtitleEn, subtitleJa) -> "Head-ache. Stom-ach-ache." (B+C).

#### L786 How Often Do You Exercise?
- A: Morning exercise with Toto, the poster and "Walking, dancing, and playing tag all count!" are natural. Ms. Emma's "Haru, go slowly today" could be "take it easy today", but go slowly is simpler and matches ゆっくりね (single flag, kept).
- B: "How often" first appears here (tested order). once / twice / three times a week / every day is a clean ladder. The silent k, w, b, c list is correct. The Or b. running examples from the block fix are already in place and subtitleEn already matches say.
- C: "Toto will lead the exercises" reads lead as the verb (after will); fine. "Don't say the k" correctly names the letter.
- Decision: no change.

#### L787 I Go to Bed at Nine
- A: Bedtime at the inn, warm milk and the inn sign are natural. "half past nine" is understood in the US and is the core expression; seg8 explains it as nine thirty (kept). seg20 ends with "Good night, and see you!", Ms. Emma's own goodbye to the child.
- B: The sleep fact (nine to twelve hours) is correct and in the tested place. What time vs How many hours is well contrasted; 9 to 7 = about ten hours is right. "Good night" as a goodbye assumes evening; the block removed time-of-day greetings from Ms. Emma's openings.
- C: Reads well. "Sixty minutes" is the plural noun; fine.
- Decision: seg20 (say, subtitleEn, subtitleJa) "Good night, and see you!" -> "See you!" / またね！ (A+B). The story-internal "Good night" (teacherScript ending, conversation line 5, seg12 TPR) is kept.

#### L788 Healthy Habits
- A: Dr. Fern's health class and poster are natural ("Small habits make a big difference!"). seg1 "Tell good habits with ing words" is not natural English.
- B: Same flag (tell needs a listener or about). The gerund-subject rule ("Put ing on an action, and it becomes a thing") is kid-level and correct; the quiz trap "Wash your hands is important" targets the rule well. Toto answers in the teacherScript, Haru in the role-play; different layers, no contradiction.
- C: Reads well. "Good morning, toast!" is a cute vocab line (kept).
- Decision: seg1 (say, subtitleEn) "Tell good habits" -> "Talk about good habits" (A+B).

#### L789 Screen Time
- A: The rainy-day talk and the Spring News tips are balanced and not preachy (time limit with family, rest eyes every thirty minutes, screens off before bed). seg7 "Ms. Emma smiles. That's a good balance." has Ms. Emma narrating herself.
- B: Same flag; the block uses "I say, ~" (as in L787 seg7). "for one hour a day" is explained correctly. The silent l (talk, palm, calf) and silent h (honest, an honest answer) are correct.
- C: Reads well.
- Decision: seg7 (say, subtitleEn) "Ms. Emma smiles. That's a good balance." -> "I say, That's a good balance." with JA わたし「いい バランスね。」 (A+B).

#### L790 Habit Survey
- A: The survey chart and the presentation are natural. The chart numbers agree with the lines (5 breakfast, 6 of 8 sleep nine or more = most, 2 exercise, 7 wash hands).
- B: Most / Only are taught by number size and the quiz is clear. goalJa says AI の エマ先生 but the app picks Riko (wrap-up "talk with Riko"); the starter "Let's look at your survey results." works for Riko.
- C: "we present the results" / "Let's present" read present as the verb in context; fine.
- Decision: goalJa AI の エマ先生に -> AI の リコに (objective JA).

#### Block-wide notes
- **"says k" letter-name pattern**: fixed in L781-785 as "makes a k sound". Please grep W27-28 (and earlier phonics worlds if wanted) for `says k`, `say k`, `says ch`, `Hear the k` in say lines and the teacherScript. "Don't say the k" / "The K is sleeping" name the letter and are fine.
- **Ms. Emma in the third person beyond says / asks**: grep W27-28 narration (segments and teacherScript) for `Ms. Emma (tells|takes|smiles|gives|shows|helps|brings|nods|laughs)` and `with Ms. Emma`. Keep it inside letters, cards and notes written by others (L784 Dr. Fern's note "Tell Ms. Emma", L785 card signature) and in Ms. Emma's own conversation lines.
- **goalJa naming Ms. Emma as the AI partner**: the app never picks Ms. Emma (engine: "Ms. Emma は 先生なので かいわ相手に しない"). Outside my group, world-27 still has this at the advice lesson ("AI の エマ先生が あつくて つかれて いるよ", around L797-800) and the deep-breath lesson ("エマ先生（AI）と いっしょに しんこきゅう", around L801-805); world-28 has two ("エマ先生（AI）に、いま 英語で できる ことを", "エマ先生（AI）と スピーチの れんしゅう"). If the starter there is spoken as Ms. Emma, it may also need the "Let's pretend I'm Ms. Emma." frame.
- **Comma-split syllables**: L785 "Stom, ach, ache" -> hyphen beat form. Check other "Clap the parts" segments in the block.
- **"Good night" / "Sleep well tonight"** in Ms. Emma's own wrap-up: L787 "Good night, and see you!" fixed; "Sleep well tonight!" kept (tonight is still ahead at any time of day).
- Kept on purpose: L781 "What a lovely village!" and seg8 "tell how it feels"; L782 repeated "I'm tired" (core); L786 "go slowly today"; L787 "half past nine" (core) and the story's "Good night"; L788 "Good morning, toast!", Toto vs Haru answering in two layers; all "…" in conversation lines, the L784 JA hint, and the safety lines.

### Run 9 review notes: L791-800 (world 27, Stage 3)

These notes cover school expressions and grammar, vocab, TPR, phonics words and tips, conversation lines and aiTask, recap, teacherScript, and every teacher segment (say, subtitleJa, subtitleEn, slide), plus testPoints. I did not redo or undo the block-wide fixes (OK, US praise, I say, Hi/Hello openings, "Let's pretend" starters). There are 33 patches in 8 lessons; L793 and L795 have no change. There are no learning-item ID changes and no changes to school.expressions[].en, vocab[].en, tpr[].en or phonics words. The present perfect block is well built: for + length (L791), since + start (L792), both (L793-794), review (L795), and the story numbers agree across lessons (Granny Capy ten years in L791 and L793; Haru karate since seven in L792 and for three years in L795; Haru and Riko friends since kindergarten, for five years). should (L796), shouldn't (L797) and Why don't you/we (L798, first use, tested) come one step at a time, and the health advice stays at rest / water / sleep / tell a grown-up. Themes: (1) **Narration person**: two "Ms. Emma points / gives ... and says" lines escaped the block-wide I say fix (L794, L796). (2) **TTS**: the bare heteronyms "Live." (L791 vocab), "and bow." (L792) and "I read ... until midnight" (L797), the invented respellings "Fer." and "Sins." (L791, L792), and comma syllables "Kin, der, gar, ten" / "comf, ta, ble" (L792, L796). (3) **Logic and rules**: a When-question answered with "Since ~" (L794 quiz), "Value, then able" without the dropped e (L796), and "Comfortable. Able means can be." (L800; comfortable is not "can be comforted"). (4) **US usage**: the newspaper "corner" (Japanese English) -> "column" (L799), "get a good sleep" (L798), and "Sleep well tonight" in Ms. Emma's own closing (L799). Every new sentence has 15 words or fewer. The check script passes with ALL OK. All changed lessons are 208-223 s long with 100% English, and every slide item is valid.

#### L791 I've Lived Here for Ten Years
- A: Granny Capy's story by the fire, the photo note and the tea talk are warm, natural US English. "Good night, everyone. Sweet dreams at the Spring Inn!" closes a story set at night at the inn (story-internal, kept).
- B: "I've lived here means from then until now. For ten years tells how long" is the right rule. seg9 "Fer." is an invented respelling. seg2 starts and ends with the bare vocab word "Live." / "Live!".
- C: "Live." alone can be read /laɪv/; "Fer." may come out as "F-E-R" or an odd word. "short and soft, like fur" uses a real word with the same weak sound.
- Decision: seg2 (say, subtitleEn) -> "I live, you live. Your home is in a place. I live in a little house. I live here!" with JA to match (B+C). seg9 (say, subtitleEn) "short and soft. Fer." -> "short and soft, like fur." (B+C; the JA 「ファ」 already fits).

#### L792 Since I Was Five
- A: The rainy-day lobby, the piano and the Rainy Day Show poster are natural. "Riko, that was lovely!" is fine in US English for a song (kept). The core "What have you done for a long time?" is understandable but a native would more often say "What's something you've done for a long time?" (tested core; reported, kept).
- B: "Since tells the start" is correct and the for/since quiz is good. seg9 "Sins." is a respelling with a z sound and a meaning that is odd for kids. teacherScript "Kin, der, gar, ten." should use the hyphen beat form.
- C: seg4 "Punch, kick, and bow." ends on a bare heteronym; TTS may say "bo" (a bow and arrow). "It rhymes with prince" gives the exact /ɪns/ sound.
- Decision: seg4 (say, subtitleEn, subtitleJa) "and bow." -> "and bow to your teacher." / せんせいに おじぎ (B+C). seg9 (say, subtitleEn) "Sins." -> "It rhymes with prince." (B+C). teacherScript "Kin, der, gar, ten." -> "Kin-der-gar-ten." (B+C).

#### L793 How Long Have You Known Her?
- A: The foot spring talk, Riko's tease "He was always so noisy!" and the Spring News note about Granny Capy and Dr. Fern are natural and fun. The vocab "foot spring" is a coined word; a US speaker would say "foot bath" (learning item used in 8 fields of the world file; reported, kept).
- B: "Since plus the start. For plus how long." and "Riko is a girl, so say her." are clear. The numbers agree with L791 (ten years) and with Haru's kindergarten line (five years). The quiz trap "since five years" is a good one.
- C: Reads well. "Know!" is fine (same sound as no, but the slide shows know).
- Decision: no change.

#### L794 I've Been Here Since Monday
- A: The calendar, Kiko's guest-book message and Birimo's cookies are natural. teacherScript "Ms. Emma points to Monday and says" and seg6 "Ms. Emma points." are Ms. Emma talking about herself in the third person (the block-wide fix changed these to "I"). seg18 asks "When did Haru start to feel better?" but the choices are "Since Monday / Since Wednesday"; a When-question takes "On ~".
- B: Same two flags. "Haru has felt much better since Wednesday" is correct (has for one person; seg8 says so). The JA すいようびから ずっと げんきに なった is fine.
- C: "Ms. Emma points. Look at the calendar." is hard to voice as the teacher herself.
- Decision: teacherScript -> "I point to Monday and say, We've been here since Monday." (A+B). seg6 (say, subtitleEn) -> "I point and say, Look at the calendar." with JA わたしが ゆびさして (A+B). seg18 (say, subtitleEn, subtitleJa) choices -> "A, On Monday. B, On Wednesday." (A+B); seg19 "It's B! Haru has felt much better since Wednesday." still answers it.

#### L795 My Long-Time Hobby
- A: Hobby Night, Haru's speech card (dojo, yellow belt, black belt dream) and "It's hard, but I love it" are real US kid talk. "Riko, you have a new song" is a little plain ("you're learning a new song") but fine (single flag, kept). "What's your hobby?" in the starter is a known keeper.
- B: The review of for/since, silent b (thumb) and ch = k (echo) is correct. The numbers agree with L792 (karate since seven, now three years).
- C: seg13 "Bow! Kick! Thumbs up! Clap! Bow!" has a bare "Bow!"; in a TPR speed list right after "Bow like a karate student" it should read as the verb (single flag, kept; listen if TTS says "bo").
- Decision: no change.

#### L796 You Should Drink Water
- A: The hot mountain path, the trail sign and the advice are natural and safe ("Small sips are best", hat, shade). teacherScript "Ms. Emma gives her a water bottle and says" is third-person self-narration.
- B: Same flag. "We use should for good advice" is right. teacherScript "Valuable. Value, then able." leaves out the dropped e that seg10 and tipJa teach. "comf, ta, ble" should match seg10's "COMF-ta-ble".
- C: The comma syllables "comf, ta, ble" read as three odd words; the hyphen form reads as one word with a beat.
- Decision: teacherScript (one patch): "I give her a water bottle and say,"; "Say it short, Comf-ta-ble."; "Valuable. Value, drop the e, able." (A+B).

#### L797 You Shouldn't Skip Breakfast
- A: Morning exercises, the skipped breakfast and Dr. Fern's tips poster are natural and kind. "Here, have a rice ball." is a warm touch.
- B: "Should not, shouldn't. It's gentle advice." and the thumbs up/down are clear. The -able tips (enjoyable, readable, believable, reliable with y to i) are correct. Haru's line "I read my comic book until midnight" is past, and the JA よんで いたんだ is past continuous.
- C: "I read my comic book until midnight" can be read as present /riːd/ (a habit). "Haru read his comic book" (third person, seg18 and teacherScript) is safe and kept.
- Decision: conversation.lines[5], seg7 (say, subtitleEn, slide.items[1]) "I read my comic book until midnight." -> "I was reading my comic book until midnight." (B+C).

#### L798 Why Don't You Take a Break?
- A: Riko studying too long, Granny Capy's study room rules and the walk to the square are natural. teacherScript "Kiko looks at the clock. Riko, you've studied for three hours!" has no speaker verb, so it sounds like narration. Rule five "get a good sleep every night" is not idiomatic.
- B: "Why don't you is a gentle idea. Why don't we means, let's do it together!" is the right contrast (first use at L798, tested). The -able tips are correct.
- C: Same flag on the missing "says": the listener hears Ms. Emma call Riko. "Riko smiles. That's a good idea." in seg7 works because the line is clearly hers (single flag, kept).
- Decision: teacherScript (one patch): "Kiko looks at the clock and says, Riko, ..."; "Five, sleep well every night." (A+C).

#### L799 Advice Column
- A: Sleepy Squirrel's letter and the advice are natural and correct (screens keep you awake, a calm book helps). But the newspaper section is called a "corner" (teacherScript, seg0, conversation line 0, scene). That is Japanese English (コーナー); the lesson title already says "Advice Column". "Sleep well tonight, everyone!" closes Ms. Emma's own talk and assumes the child's evening.
- B: Same flags. "What should I do? asks for advice." is hard to follow as a sentence. The -able tips (keep the e in changeable / noticeable, double t in unforgettable) are correct.
- C: "What should I do? asks for advice" stops mid-sentence when read aloud; "To ask for advice, say, What should I do?" reads cleanly.
- Decision: teacherScript (one patch): "advice corner" -> "advice column", "To ask for advice, say, What should I do?", "Sleep well, everyone!" (A+B). conversation.scene, conversation.lines[0].en, seg0 (say, subtitleEn) "corner" -> "column" (A+B; JA コーナー kept, it is natural Japanese).

#### L800 Advice Show
- A: The 800th lesson party, the poster and the Advice Show are warm and natural. Riko's headache advice (rest, water, tell Dr. Fern) and seg8 "always tell a grown-up" are safe health language.
- B: "Because gives the reason" is right. seg9 "Comfortable. Able means can be." and tipJa "comfortable の -able は「～できる」" are wrong: comfortable does not mean "can be comforted". L796 teaches comfortable for its stress (COMF-ta-ble), so the review should do the same.
- C: "COMF-ta-ble" matches L796 seg10, so the review sounds the same.
- Decision: seg9 (say, subtitleEn, subtitleJa) "Comfortable. Able means can be." -> "Comfortable. Say it short, COMF-ta-ble." (A+B). phonics.tipJa -> "comfortable は COMF-ta-ble と みじかく いう。" (A+B).

#### Block-wide notes
- **Third-person Ms. Emma in narration**: the block-wide "Ms. Emma says / asks" fix missed other verbs. Please grep W27-28 teacherScript and segment `say` for `Ms\. Emma (points|gives|smiles|claps|shows|looks|holds|takes|puts|opens)` and JA `エマ先生が` (fixed here in L794, L796).
- **Bare heteronyms at vocab edges**: "Live." (L791), "and bow." (L792), "I read ... until midnight" (L797). Worth a grep for `\b(Live|Bow|Read|Close|Present|Record|Wind|Tear|Lead|Minute)[.!]` at segment start/end and first-person "I read" in past contexts.
- **Invented sound spellings**: "Fer." and "Sins." fixed. Grep the block for other one-word respellings after "short" / "Say" (e.g. "Becuz", "Wuz", "Ta").
- **Comma syllables**: "Kin, der, gar, ten" and "comf, ta, ble" -> hyphen form. Same pattern may appear in other teacherScript phonics endings.
- **comfortable as an -able "can" word**: L800 fixed; check L806-810 reviews for the same claim.
- **"corner" for a newspaper section / TV segment**: L799 fixed; world-38 also has "advice corner" (outside this block). W28 has the title "Opinion Corner" (L825, tested title), which is fine as a classroom corner but worth a look.
- **Coined vocab "foot spring"** (L793, learning item): US English is "foot bath". Not changed; if you change it, it touches vocab[3], seg5, teacherScript, scene and conversation in L793 and other L79x fields (world-27.json lines 6147-6298).
- **Core "What have you done for a long time?"** (L792, tested core): understandable; a more natural question is "What's something you've done for a long time?". Kept.
- Kept on purpose: L791 "Good night, everyone. Sweet dreams at the Spring Inn!" (story set at night at the inn); L792 "that was lovely" and Granny's "what a lovely song"; L795 "Riko, you have a new song" and the speed TPR "Bow!"; L798 seg7 "Riko smiles. That's a good idea."; capital stress words (TEN YEARS, KINDERGARTEN, COMF-ta-ble) as the house stress style; all "…" in conversation lines.

### Run 9 review notes: L801-810 (world 27, Stage 3)

These notes cover school expressions and grammar, vocab, TPR, phonics words and tips, conversation lines and aiTask, recap, teacherScript, and every teacher segment (say, subtitleJa, subtitleEn, slide), plus testPoints. I did not redo or undo the block-wide fixes (OK, US praise, "I say", Hi/Hello openings, plain definitions, "Let's pretend" starters, pen pal). There are 35 patches in 8 lessons; L801 and L803 have no change. There are no learning-item ID changes and no changes to school.expressions[].en, vocab[].en, tpr[].en or phonics words. The feelings arc (worried L801, deep breaths L802, sad L803, kind words L804, calm-down plan L805) is warm, kind and correct, and the health facts are right (nine to twelve hours of sleep, wash hands, tell an adult; no medicine). Themes: (1) **Story logic**: in L807 Riko the host holds the bell, but then Haru "rings the bell"; in L809 Ms. Emma says the rainbow "is" the Rainbow Bridge, while the teacherScript and the story plan say it leads there. (2) **TTS**: "Say for short and soft" (L805, L807, L809) sounds like the idiom "for short"; "I. Walk. Slowly." (L802); bare "ch" in the teacherScript phonics lines (L806, L808, L810; the segments already say "C H"); "You should gives good advice" (L809). (3) **Leftovers from block-wide steps**: "Pen friend" in L808 seg5 say/subtitleEn (vocab, slide and JA were already pen pal); "Lovely!" in the L804 teacherScript; "So Ms. Emma teaches everyone" in the L802 teacherScript (third person in her own narration). (4) **Small wording**: "a vegetable soup. A big one!" (L806), "Let's play a health quiz" (L807 starter), "You touch it on your face" (L802 quiz), "Let's find out together!" with nothing to find out (L805), and the L810 wrap-up that goes on after "Bye-bye!". Every new sentence has 15 words or fewer. The check script passes with ALL OK. All changed lessons are 211-226 s long with 100% English, and every slide item is valid.

#### L801 When I'm Worried
- A: Kiko by the window, the poster "Feeling Worried?" and "Talking to someone you trust really helps" are natural and kind. "Hold your hands. You feel worried." (TPR) is a little odd for clasping your own hands ("Hold your hands together" / "Squeeze your hands"), but it is a learning item and the action makes it clear (single flag, reported).
- B: When I'm worried, I ~ / What do you do when you're worried? are clear and at level. The week/weak and here/hear explanations are correct ("There's an ear in hear" is a good memory hook). EN/JA match.
- C: Reads well. "Blah, blah, blah" and "Ahh, warm water" are fine.
- Decision: no change.

#### L802 Deep Breaths
- A: The Heart Spring sign and the breathing steps are what a teacher really says. The teacherScript says "So Ms. Emma teaches everyone a calm-down trick" inside Ms. Emma's own narration. seg18 "You touch it on your face" is not natural English.
- B: Same flag on seg18 (the JA already says かおに あって さわれる). breath vs breathe ("Breath is the thing. Breathe is what you do") is clear and correct. The knows silent-k tip is correct; the JA hint いきを する is one of the two allowed.
- C: The third-person "Ms. Emma teaches" sounds odd in her own voice. seg5 "I. Walk. Slowly." — "I." alone may be read as a letter or "the first".
- Decision: teacherScript "So Ms. Emma teaches everyone" -> "So I teach everyone" (A+C). seg5 (say, subtitleEn, subtitleJa) "I. Walk. Slowly." -> "I... walk... slowly." / わたしは… ゆっくり… あるく。 (A+C). seg18 (say, subtitleEn) "You touch it on your face." -> "It's on your face. You can touch it." (A+B).

#### L803 It's OK to Feel Sad
- A: Toto alone in the square, "It's OK to feel sad sometimes" and the Feelings Corner ("tell a grown-up in your family or a teacher") are kind and natural.
- B: It's OK to ~ / looks sad / You can ~ are introduced well. flour/flower and whole/hole (silent w) are correct. The JA かのじょ in expression 2 is a keeper.
- C: Reads well. The spelled quiz choices "F L O U R" / "F L O W E R" are intended letter spell-outs (kept).
- Decision: no change.

#### L804 Kind Words
- A: The Kind Words Wall and "Mistakes help me learn" are good. The teacherScript praise "Lovely!" is British. The conversation jumps from "start again" straight to "You did your best" (the teacherScript says she finishes; single flag, kept).
- B: Same flag on "Lovely!" (block rule: US praise). right/write and pair/pear are correct.
- C: Reads well.
- Decision: teacherScript "Lovely!" -> "Wonderful!" (A+B).

#### L805 My Calm-Down Plan
- A: The plans and Birimo's "Mr. Fluff" are fun and natural. seg0 ends "Let's find out together!", but there is nothing to find out; the lesson makes plans. "Let's enjoy a calm, happy week!" has an object, so it is fine (kept).
- B: Same flag on seg0 (JA いっしょに しらべよう also doesn't fit). seg9 "Say for short and soft" is unclear for a learner: is "for" the word or the preposition? The weak/-able/silent l review is correct.
- C: "Say for short" sounds like the idiom "for short".
- Decision: seg0 (say, subtitleEn, subtitleJa) "Let's find out together!" -> "Let's make them together!" / いっしょに つくろう！ (A+B). seg9 (say, subtitleEn) "Say for short and soft." -> "The word for is short and soft." (same wording as the teacherScript; B+C).

#### L806 A Healthy Week Plan
- A: Haru's plan is a realistic kid plan. Birimo's "I'm going to cook a vegetable soup. A big one!" is not native ("a vegetable soup"). "Turn your wrists round and round" is fine in US speech (kept).
- B: Same flag (soup is uncountable here), and the JA おおきな おなべで says "a big pot", not "a big one". On ~day, I'm going to ~ is clear. ch=k, silent w, since, -able, here/hear are correct.
- C: The teacherScript says "The ch sounds like k" with a bare "ch"; the segments already say "C H".
- Decision: conversation.lines[4], seg7 (say, subtitleEn), seg7 slide.items[1] "cook a vegetable soup. A big one!" -> "cook vegetable soup. A big pot!" (A+B). teacherScript "The ch sounds like k." -> "The C H sounds like k." (B+C).

#### L807 Health Quiz
- A: The quiz cards ("Washing your hands helps stop germs", "When you feel sick, you should tell an adult") are correct and kind. In the teacherScript, Riko (the host) holds the bell, then "Haru rings the bell". The starter "Let's play a health quiz" is Japanese-English.
- B: Same story flag; same flag on "play a quiz" (クイズを する -> do a quiz). Nine to twelve hours of sleep is correct (tested). seg10 "Say for short and soft" (same as L805). "Germs. Tiny, tiny bugs." is fine colloquial US.
- C: "Say for short" again. "Say for softly" in the teacherScript has no idiom problem (kept).
- Decision: teacherScript "Haru rings the bell and answers" -> "Haru raises his hand and answers" (A+B). seg10 (say, subtitleEn) -> "The word for is short and soft." (B+C). aiTask.starterEn "Let's play a health quiz." -> "Let's do a health quiz." (frame "Let's pretend I'm Dr. Fern." unchanged; A+B).

#### L808 A Letter of Advice
- A: Kiko's letter is a good model (Dear / You should / It's good for / Your friend). "Dear friend, How are you?" keeps the capital as the letter's next line (kept). seg5 still says "Pen friend" in say and subtitleEn.
- B: Same flag: the vocab item, slide and JA already say pen pal (block-wide). character, salmon, since April, valuable, write are correct.
- C: Bare "ch" in the teacherScript (same as L806).
- Decision: seg5 (say, subtitleEn) "Pen friend ... Pen friend!" -> "Pen pal ... Pen pal!" (objective). teacherScript "The ch sounds like k." -> "The C H sounds like k." (B+C).

#### L809 Village of Springs Review
- A: The goodbye walk and the Spring News are natural; the festival timing agrees with L807 ("in two days") and L810. Ms. Emma's line "Maybe it's the Rainbow Bridge!" makes the rainbow itself the bridge, but the teacherScript says "Maybe it leads to the Rainbow Bridge!" (story plan: the rainbow leads to the bridge). seg8 "What's wrong is a kind question" reads as a sentence.
- B: Same story flag. seg8 "You should gives good advice" is grammatical only as a word mention and confuses learners. "For tells how long" is correct. seg9 "Say for short and soft" (same as L805).
- C: seg8 trips the reader at "What's wrong is" and "should gives". seg9 "for short" idiom.
- Decision: conversation.lines[5] (en, ja) "Maybe it's the Rainbow Bridge!" -> "Maybe it leads to the Rainbow Bridge!" / もしかしたら にじの はしに つづいて いるのかも！ (A+B; Rainbow Bridge test still passes). seg8 (say, subtitleEn, subtitleJa) -> "What's wrong? That's a kind question. Use You should to give advice." (A+C). seg9 (say, subtitleEn) -> "The word for is short and soft." (B+C).

#### L810 World 27 Grand Review
- A: The festival program, the speech and "Thank you for everything, Granny Capy!" are warm and natural. "At half past ten / half past eleven" is understood in the US but "ten thirty" is more common for kids (single flag, kept). The wrap-up says "Bye-bye!" and then goes on with a second "Next is World 28".
- B: The world review is complete and correct (echo, headache, knee, since kindergarten, unforgettable, week). The speed TPR "Look! Stop! Come here! Go!" is in place (tested).
- C: Talking after "Bye-bye!" and two "Next" in a row are awkward to read. "Bow to Granny Capy" reads correctly as bau in this frame.
- Decision: teacherScript "The ch sounds like k." -> "The C H sounds like k." (B+C). seg20 (say, subtitleEn, subtitleJa) -> "... World 28, the Rainbow Bridge, is coming up! Next, go and talk with Riko! Bye-bye!" with JA もうすぐ ワールド28、にじの はしだよ！ first (A+C; the "Next, go and talk with Riko!" partner line is unchanged).

#### Block-wide notes
- **"Say for short and soft"**: fixed in L805, L807, L809 ("The word for is short and soft."). The same line may appear in other for/since lessons (L791-795) and in W28 reviews; grep `Say for short`.
- **Bare "ch" in teacherScript phonics lines**: "The ch sounds like k." fixed in L806, L808, L810 -> "The C H sounds like k." (the segments already use "C H"). Probably also in L781-784 and L790/L800; grep `The ch sounds`.
- **Third person in Ms. Emma's own teacherScript narration**: the block-wide step caught "Ms. Emma says / asks" but not other verbs ("So Ms. Emma teaches everyone", L802). Grep teacherScripts for `Ms\. Emma [a-z]+s ` outside quotes.
- **British praise in teacherScript**: "Lovely!" alone (L804) was missed by the "Lovely work!" step; grep `Lovely!` / `Brilliant!` in W27-28 teacherScripts.
- **Pen friend leftovers**: L808 seg5 say/subtitleEn still said "Pen friend"; grep `[Pp]en friend` across W27-28 say/subtitleEn.
- **"half past ten"** (L810 program) is kept; consider "ten thirty" block-wide if you want strictly US clock talk.
- Kept on purpose: L801 TPR "Hold your hands. You feel worried." (learning item; reported), L802 "my nose smells cake", L803 letter spell-outs, L804 conversation jump to "You did your best", L805 "Let's enjoy a calm, happy week!", L806 "round and round", L807 "Say for softly" and "Tiny, tiny bugs", L808 "Dear friend, How are you?", L810 "I did my speech!" and "half past"; all "…" and the L802 JA hint.

### Run 9 review notes: L811-820 (world 28, Stage 3)

These notes cover school expressions and grammar, vocab, TPR, phonics words and tips, conversation lines and aiTask, recap, teacherScript, every teacher segment (say, subtitleJa, subtitleEn, slide) and testPoints. I did not redo or undo the block-wide fixes (OK, fall, US praise, "I say", Hi/Hello openings, plain definitions, "Let's pretend I'm ..." starters). There are 39 patches in 9 lessons. L820 has no change. There are no learning-item ID changes and no changes to school.expressions[].en, vocab[].en, tpr[].en or phonics words. The opening of the Rainbow Bridge is warm and natural. The grammar order holds: no "is a kind of" before L816, no "used for" before L817, no relative clause with who/that in the tested fields. The culture facts check out: Finland shoes off at home, a January school year in Kenya, late August in Mexico, school at seven and free lunch in Finland, Tanabata in summer, snow festivals in the north. The rainbow story is consistent too (red back in L815, orange in L820, "Two colors are back"). Themes: (1) **Ms. Emma in the third person**: "Ms. Emma smiles / laughs" in segment narration (L812, L816, L817) and a JA エマせんせい「 left after "I say" (L811, L814). (2) **Facts and story**: "Idea. i-DE-a. Not ai-de-a." (L813; English idea does start with "ai"), Riko brings the furoshiki in seg0 but it is Kiko's everywhere else (L817), "Bento is a kind of lunch box" (L816), counting "One, two, three" when one color is back (L815). (3) **TTS**: "ka-ra-O-ke" / "ka-ri-O-ki" (L818) became the real-word beats "carry-O-key". (4) **Small wording**: "In Japan, we, is for ..." (L812), "Line up!" for lining up shoes (L812), "eats with hands" (L814), "Long water that flows" (L819), and the relative-clause uniform definition (L813). Every new sentence has 15 words or fewer. The check script passes with ALL OK. All touched lessons are 220-228 s long with 100% English, and every slide item is valid.

#### L811 A Rainbow Bridge
- A: Iris, the sign at the bridge gate and "This bridge connects many countries" are natural. Ms. Emma's "Then let's make new friends and bring the colors back!" is a good hook. seg2 "Make a bridge with your arm" (one arm can make an arch; single flag, kept).
- B: The connects-with-s point and the why question are clear. The katakana stress contrast (EN-er-gy, not e-ne-ru-gi) is correct. seg7 EN says "I say, Then let's ...", but the JA still says エマせんせい「...」: EN/JA mismatch left over from the block-wide change.
- C: Reads well. "VI-ta-min" may be read by TTS as the Roman numeral VI. I kept it because it is the house beat form (sounds = VI is pinned), and I flag it below. "Bow to Iris" / "Bow!" in the speed challenge are clear from the TPR context.
- Decision: seg7 subtitleJa エマせんせい「 -> わたし「 (objective JA).

#### L812 Same or Different?
- A: The plaza, Moi / Jambo / Hola and the shoes talk are natural. seg7 "Ms. Emma smiles. Every family is different." is the teacher talking about herself in the third person. seg8 "In Japan, we, is for many people in Japan" sounds like a grammar slip. seg13 "Line up!" tells kids to stand in a line, but the command is to line up the shoes.
- B: Same flags on seg7 (block-wide style is "I ...") and seg13 (the speed word should keep the meaning of "Line them up neatly"). In the teacherScript, Iris says "Every family is different" and in the conversation it is Ms. Emma. That is a different telling, not a contradiction (single flag, kept). The culture facts are fine.
- C: seg8 "we, is" trips the reader. "Slide, slide" and "Tap, tap your feet" are fine.
- Decision: seg7 (say, subtitleEn) "Ms. Emma smiles. Every family ..." -> "I smile and say, Every family ...", JA わたしは にっこり (A+B). seg8 (say, subtitleEn) "In Japan, we, is for ... In my family, we, is for your home." -> "In Japan, we means many people in Japan. In my family, we means the people in your home.", JA じぶんの いえの ひとの こと (A+C). seg13 (say, subtitleEn) "Line up!" -> "Line them up!" (A+B).

#### L813 How About in Your Country?
- A: The school talk is natural ("That's cool! Do you clean the windows, too?"), and so is Amara's school card. seg4 "Clothes that everyone at a school wears" is a stiff, dictionary-style definition.
- B: The facts are right (Kenya: January; Mexico: late August; Finland: school at seven). seg4 uses a relative clause (Stage 4); the block-wide fix moved definitions like this to plain sentences. In the teacherScript, "Idea. i-DE-a. Not ai-de-a." is wrong: English idea does begin with the "ai" sound (/aɪˈdiːə/). The real contrast is the four flat katakana beats a-i-de-a, the same frame as "Not e-ne-ru-gi" (L811) and "Not ka-me-ra" (L812).
- C: Reads well. TTS reads "i-DE-a" with "I", which is the right sound.
- Decision: seg4 (say, subtitleEn, subtitleJa) -> "Uniform. Everyone at a school wears the same clothes." with JA がっこうの みんなが おなじ ふくを きるよ。 (A+B). teacherScript "Not ai-de-a." -> "Not a-i-de-a." (objective).

#### L814 That's Interesting!
- A: The lunch scene, Mateo's tortillas and "Let's try it your way, Mateo! Hands are easy!" are natural and kind. seg18 "Your friend eats with hands" is missing the possessive.
- B: Same flag on seg18; the core says "with my hands". The culture facts are right (tortillas by hand, free school lunch in Finland, fish soup, chapati). seg7 EN says "I say", but the JA still says エマせんせい「. The one Japanese hint in the teacherScript (ちがう やりかたで) is allowed. seg2 "Something new that you want to know more about" is fine (single flag, kept).
- C: Reads well. "Open, close, open, close" is the verb close; fine.
- Decision: seg7 subtitleJa エマせんせい「 -> わたし「 (objective JA). seg18 (say, subtitleEn) "eats with hands" -> "eats with their hands" (A+B).

#### L815 Culture Comparison Chart
- A: The chart and "In both countries, people celebrate New Year" work. In US English, "celebrate New Year's" / "the New Year" and "at New Year's" (seg17 "What do you do at New Year?") are more usual. The core is pinned by the tests, so I kept it (reported below). seg12 counts "One, two, three!" for the colors, but only one color is back.
- B: Same story flag; the teacherScript ends "One color is back. Six more to go!". Counting the seven rainbow colors fits the TPR and the story. Finland's school starts in August, which is correct, and "take off their shoes" agrees with L812.
- C: Reads well. "In blank countries" in the quiz is house style.
- Decision: seg12 (say, subtitleEn, subtitleJa) "One, two, three!" -> "Seven colors in all. One, two, three, four, five, six, seven!" with JA ぜんぶで 7いろ。 (A+B).

#### L816 It's a Kind of Rice Ball
- A: Onigiri, the food cards and Birimo's four onigiri are fun and natural, and the count adds up (Try one + three more = four). seg7 has "Ms. Emma laughs." in the third person. seg17 "Bento is a kind of lunch box": in US English, a lunch box is the container, and a bento is the boxed meal. It is also not "a food you like".
- B: Same flags. In the teacherScript, "Bento. It's a lunch box." has the same problem. Miso soup (on Kiko's food card) gives a clean "is a kind of" example. The spelling tips (shi, fu, m before p) are correct. The quiz slide shows "salmon" next to option B "a kind of fish". That is a little loose, but it is a valid lesson item (single flag, kept).
- C: Reads well. "SU-shi. TO-fu. BEN-to." are fine.
- Decision: seg7 (say, subtitleEn) "Ms. Emma laughs. Birimo, ..." -> "I laugh and say, Birimo, ...", JA わたしは わらって (A+B). seg17 (say, subtitleEn, subtitleJa) -> "For example, Miso soup is a kind of soup!" with JA みそしるは スープの いっしゅだよ (A+B). teacherScript "It's a lunch box." -> "It's a boxed lunch." (A+B).

#### L817 It's Used for Wrapping
- A: The furoshiki and "Can you wrap me in it? I'm a present!" are natural and fun. seg0 says "Look what Riko brought", but the furoshiki is Kiko's in the teacherScript and the conversation ("Kiko made a bag!"). seg7 has "Ms. Emma laughs." in the third person.
- B: The seg0 story contradiction is objective. "Used for, plus an ing word" and "Made of tells us the material" are correct, and so are the f / j spelling tips. The teacherScript has "o-ri-GA-mi" and seg9 has "or-i-GA-mi"; both are acceptable (kept).
- C: Reads well. "Wrap a present" / "I'm a present" use the noun; fine.
- Decision: seg0 (say, subtitleEn, subtitleJa) Riko -> Kiko (objective). seg7 (say, subtitleEn) "Ms. Emma laughs. You're too big ..." -> "I laugh and say, You're too big ...", JA わたしは わらって (A+B).

#### L818 You Can See It in Spring
- A: The four seasons, Riko's season guide and "Every season is noodle season!" are natural. Tanabata in summer and snow festivals in the north are correct. "I think fall is the best" is fine.
- B: seg9 gives karaoke as "ka-ra-O-ke" (the Japanese syllables). The teacherScript gives "ka-ri-O-ki" and tipJa gives キャリオウキ, so the three disagree. US English is /ˌkæriˈoʊki/.
- C: TTS will read "O-ke" like "okay" and "ki" unpredictably. The real-word beats "carry-O-key" read right and match tipJa.
- Decision: seg9 (say, subtitleEn, subtitleJa) and teacherScript karaoke beats -> "carry-O-key" (B+C).

#### L819 Let Me Tell You About Japan
- A: Town talk, Birimo's cake-shop joke and "This bridge is your town now, Birimo!" are warm. The address safety line is good. seg3 "Long water that flows to the sea" is not natural English.
- B: Same flag on seg3, which also uses a relative clause. The j spelling tips and the anime stress (AN-i-may) are correct.
- C: seg12 ends with a bare "Bow." (and seg13 "Bow!"), a heteronym. The TPR "Bow like a judo player" just before makes the verb reading likely, so this is a single flag, kept and noted.
- Decision: seg3 (say, subtitleEn, subtitleJa) -> "River. A long line of water. It flows to the sea." with JA ながい みずの ながれ。うみへ ながれて いくよ。 (A+B).

#### L820 Japan Guide Video
- A: The guide video, the upside-down camera and "Thank you for watching! See you in Japan!" are natural. Shrine: "make a wish" and the clap-clap TPR are fine for kids.
- B: The three-part video point is clear. The orange color is second, which agrees with L815 and the teacherScript ("Two colors are back"). vocab guide = あんない while seg2 defines the person; both meanings fit the title (kept).
- C: Reads well. "Lights, camera, action!" is fine.
- Decision: no change.

#### Block-wide notes
- **Ms. Emma in the third person beyond "says / asks"**: the block-wide step missed "Ms. Emma smiles / laughs" in segment narration (fixed here in L812, L816, L817 as "I smile / I laugh and say, ...") and left the hiragana エマせんせい「 in subtitleJa after "I say" (fixed in L811, L814). Pattern to grep in all 60 lessons: `Ms\. Emma (smiles|laughs|nods|claps|looks)` in say/subtitleEn and `エマせんせい` in subtitleJa.
- **"celebrate New Year"** (L815 core, pinned by tests line ~2985): US English prefers "celebrate New Year's" or "the New Year", and seg17 "at New Year" is British. I kept it because the test pins it; change the test and core together if you want it.
- **TTS on capital beats**: "VI-ta-min" (L811 seg10, teacherScript; also anywhere vitamin appears in reviews) may be read as the Roman numeral six. Check how the TTS reads it. If it misreads, change only the say lines (the sounds field VI is pinned), for example to "Vitamin. The first part is strong."
- **Bare "Bow." / "Bow!"** in speed challenges (L811, L819): this is a heteronym. Kept because the TPR context comes first; worth a listen.
- **Quiz slides** in L816-818 show a vocab word (salmon / wrap / season) next to a sentence option (B). They are valid items but a loose match; I left them alone.
- Kept on purpose: L811 "Make a bridge with your arm"; the L812 teacherScript has Iris say "Every family is different"; L814 "Something new that you want to know more about"; L815 "In blank countries"; L817 origami beats "o-ri" vs "or-i"; L818 SA-mu-rai vs SAM-u-rai; all "…", the L814 JA hint and the guest greetings.

### Run 9 review notes: L821-830 (world 28, Stage 3)

These notes cover school expressions and grammar, vocab, TPR, phonics words and tips, conversation lines and aiTask, recap, teacherScript, and every teacher segment (say, subtitleJa, subtitleEn, slide), plus testPoints. I did not redo or undo the block-wide fixes (OK, fall, soccer, pen pal, US praise, "I say", Hi/Hello openings, "Let's pretend I'm ~" starters). There are 41 patches in 10 lessons; every lesson has at least one change. There are no learning-item ID changes and no changes to school.expressions[].en, vocab[].en, tpr[].en or phonics words. The opinion ladder is in order and passes the order rules: I think ~ because ~ (L821), In my opinion first in L822, Do you agree? first in L823, First / Second (L824), the speech (L825), then the Stage 3 reviews (L828 story and mystery, L829 experiences with since, L830 made of / used for / should). The Leo video call has its 8 lines, and the safety line about address and school name is kept. Themes: (1) **TTS and sound spellings** (the main issue): the linking-sound and weak-form segments used invented solid respellings: "uv" (L822, may be read U.V.), "fer" (L824), "Meechu / coodju / woodju / didju" (L826), "Bustop / Taycare / Gooday / Hotea" (L828), "Donchu / Thissummer" (L829), and "Aw-lover" (L829, reads as "lover", wrong vowel). The bare heteronym "Bow!" was in the L827 speed challenge. (2) **Meaning**: "Finally means a long wait" (L826); "The first part is strong" right after Or-i-GA-mi (L830; that is true only for video); "What a kind lesson!" (L827). (3) **Read-aloud rhythm**: "Small words like and, to, for, and can" (L821; and is both a list word and the joiner), the same list shape in L822, "Say, Yes, I agree, or, I see your point, but, and your idea." (L823), "Maybe, Second, ..." (L825). Every new sentence has 15 words or fewer. The check script passes with ALL OK. All lessons are 210-226 s long with 100% English, and every slide item is valid.

#### L821 I Think… Because…
- A: The seasons board and the plaza talk are natural US classroom English. "Is it the sea or the ice cream?" is a fun follow-up. seg6 drops "Is it" from Kiko's line (the slide shows the full line); fine for a narrated retell (single flag, kept). Mateo's birthday in October and Amara's rainy season are fine.
- B: I think ~ because ~ and Why do you think so? ― Because ~ are taught one step at a time, and the quiz (Because I love snow vs Yes, I do) checks the right thing. seg8 "Small words like and, to, for, and can are short and soft": the learner cannot tell the list word "and" from the joining "and".
- C: Same flag on seg8; read aloud it sounds like "for and can". The all-caps stress words (SUMMER, SEA, BEST) follow the house pattern for rhythm lessons (see block-wide notes).
- Decision: seg8 (say, subtitleEn) "Small words like and, to, for, and can are short and soft." -> "Small words are short and soft. And, to, for, can." with the JA order to match (B+C).

#### L822 In My Opinion
- A: The lunch menu card, serving lunch to classmates, itadakimasu, "Can I have seconds?" are natural and true to Japan. Aino's "lunch is free for every student" in Finland is correct. Aino says "we should have more time to eat" in the script and Haru says it in the conversation; these are two retellings (kept).
- B: "In my opinion means I think. It's a polite way to start." is a clear rule, and the first use is in L822 (tested). seg8 "Of is so small, it sounds like uv" uses an invented respelling, and the list "of, at, or, and to" has the same shape as L821.
- C: "uv" is a TTS risk (may be read U.V.). Modeling the phrase again is easier and more useful.
- Decision: seg8 (say, subtitleEn) -> "Sound time! Big words are strong. Small words are quick and soft. Of, at, or, to. We have a BOWL of RICE. Of is tiny. A BOWL of RICE." with JA to match (B+C).

#### L823 Do You Agree?
- A: The talking rules sign is lovely and natural ("Listen to the end. Don't stop your friend in the middle."). "a giant snow Birimo" is a nice joke. seg17 "Say, Yes, I agree, or, I see your point, but, and your idea." is a hard chain of commas.
- B: Do you agree? first appears here (tested). I'm not sure / I see your point, but ~ are kind and correct. "disagree kindly" in the wrap-up is fine. seg17 is confusing to follow (single flag from B, joins A and C).
- C: seg17 stumbles on ", or, ... but, and"; splitting it into two sentences gives a clean pause.
- Decision: seg17 (say, subtitleEn) -> "Say, Yes, I agree. Or say, I see your point, but, and your idea." (A+C).

#### L824 Two Reasons
- A: Amara's opinion card, "A bus is faster!" and "Tomorrow, I'll walk… to the snack shop." are natural and funny. "No bus today, Birimo!" ties back to the quiz option (kept).
- B: First / Second / Do you have another reason? are at level; "another = one more" is correct. seg9 "For sounds like fer" is an invented respelling, against the brief.
- C: "fer" is not a standard word for TTS; describing the weak sound is safer.
- Decision: seg9 (say, subtitleEn) "For sounds like fer." -> "For is tiny and quick." with JA for は ちいさく はやく いうよ。 (B+C).

#### L825 Opinion Corner
- A: The program (Kiko, Mateo, Aino's sauna, Birimo's snacks), "Thank you for listening. Do you agree?" and "That's a good point!" are natural speech-day language. seg19 "Maybe, Second, we can share them with friends!" is awkward.
- B: The three-part speech (opinion, First, Second) is clear; the quiz on two identical reasons is a good check. "What do you think is the best?" in the starter is open, but it suits a speech prompt (kept). NIN-ja and VI-ta-min stress are correct.
- C: "Maybe, Second," is a stumble. "VI-ta-min" / "Say the VI part" could be read as the Roman numeral VI by some TTS (single flag, kept; see block-wide).
- Decision: seg19 (say, subtitleEn, subtitleJa) "Maybe, Second, ..." -> "How about this? Second, we can share them with friends!" with JA これは どう？ (A+C).

#### L826 Video Call with a Pen Pal
- A: The video call manners card (quiet place, mute button, don't share your address or school name) and the call itself ("Can you hear me? Yes, loud and clear!", "Could you say that again?") are real, natural phrases. Leo's "It's nice to meet you, too!" for a first face-to-face talk is natural. seg8 "Finally means a long wait" is wrong.
- B: Same flag on seg8 (seg5 already says "After a long, long wait"). The t/d + you linking rule is correct, but seg9-10 spell it as "Meechu / coodju / woodju / didju".
- C: The solid respellings are TTS risks; the teacherScript already uses the hyphen forms "Meet-chu / Could-joo". Conversation has 8 lines (tested).
- Decision: seg8 (say, subtitleEn, subtitleJa) -> "Finally means after a long wait." / ながく まった あとで (A+B). seg9 (say, subtitleEn) "Meechu. Nice to meechu!" -> "Meet-chu. Nice to meet-chu!" (B+C). seg10 (say, subtitleEn) "ju ... coodju ... woodju ... didju" -> "joo ... could-joo ... would-joo ... did-joo" (B+C).

#### L827 A Gift from Japan
- A: The cranes card (one square of paper, no scissors and no glue, a thousand cranes for a wish) is correct and warm; "cranes mean long life" is a true Japanese belief. "I've made this for you" is a little British next to the US "I made this for you", but it is the tested core expression (kept, see block-wide). seg20 "What a kind lesson!" is an odd phrase (a lesson is not kind).
- B: Same flag on seg20; the JA やさしい じゅぎょう is odd too. Made of vs made for in the quiz is a good contrast. seg13 has a bare "Bow!".
- C: Bare "Bow!" in the speed challenge is a heteronym; TTS may say bow (ribbon). In seg12 "Then bow and say thank you" the verb frame reads correctly (kept). "A-kain-duv / May-duv / Pu-ti-ton / Kee-pin-touch" are hyphen forms (kept).
- Decision: seg13 (say, subtitleEn, subtitleJa) "Bow!" -> "Thank you!" / ありがとう！ (B+C). seg20 (say, subtitleEn, subtitleJa) "What a kind lesson!" -> "What a sweet story!" / すてきな おはなしだったね！ (A+B).

#### L828 Stage 3 Review: Story and Mystery
- A: The missing scarf mystery and Riko's detective notes are clear and consistent (blue thread, feathers near the lamp, peep peep, mother bird). "A baby bird is sleeping in it." has no near referent in the conversation, but the scarf is named in line 0 and it is the tested expression (kept).
- B: First / Suddenly / It must be are recycled correctly ("almost sure"). The same-sound rule is right. seg9-10 use "Bustop / Taycare / Gooday / Hotea".
- C: These respellings are TTS risks ("Hotea" may be ho-TEE-uh). The teacherScript already says "Say the s sound only once / One k sound", which is clearer.
- Decision: seg9 (say, subtitleEn, subtitleJa) "Bustop! Take care. Taycare." -> "Say one s. Take care. Say one k." (B+C). seg10 (say, subtitleEn, subtitleJa) "Gooday ... Hotea." -> "Say one d. ... Say one t." The JA keeps the katakana sound and adds ～を 1かいだけ (B+C).

#### L829 Stage 3 Review: Experiences
- A: The experience bingo card, "No, I haven't. But I've made tacos many times!" and Aino's "You should come to Finland!" are natural. "There's snow all over in winter" is colloquial but fine (it carries the phonics phrase).
- B: Have you ever / never / since are explained correctly (since = when it started). The quiz "I've ever seen snow" vs "I've never seen snow" is a good trap. seg9-10 use "Donchu" and "Thissummer", and "Aw-lover".
- C: "Aw-lover" reads as "lover" (all over = awl OH-ver); "Thissummer" is invented. The teacherScript has the same "Aw-lover".
- Decision: seg9 (say, subtitleEn) "Donchu ... donchu?" -> "Don-chu ... don-chu?" (B+C). seg10 (say, subtitleEn) "Aw-lover ... Thissummer." -> "Aw-low-ver ... This summer!" (B+C). teacherScript "Aw-lover" -> "Aw-low-ver" (B+C).

#### L830 Stage 3 Review: Explain and Advise
- A: The kendama label (made of wood, a ball on a string and three cups, ten minutes a day) is correct. "It's used for playing" is a weak description (every toy is for playing), but it is a learning item (kept). TPR "Catch the ball on the cup!" vs the label's "catch it in a cup" is a small mismatch; "on the cup" is real kendama talk (kept).
- B: made of / used for / should are recycled well; "Used for tells what it does" is close enough (single flag, kept). seg10 says "Or-i-GA-mi. And video. VID-e-o. The first part is strong." The last sentence seems to cover origami too, which is stressed on GA.
- C: Same ambiguity; naming video makes it clear. "VID-e-o" reads well.
- Decision: seg10 (say, subtitleEn, subtitleJa) "The first part is strong." -> "In video, the first part is strong." / video は さいしょが つよいよ。 (objective).

#### Block-wide notes
- **Invented solid respellings in linking/weak-form segments**: fixed here for "uv", "fer", "Meechu", "coodju/woodju/didju", "Bustop/Taycare/Gooday/Hotea", "Donchu", "Thissummer", "Aw-lover". The same strings remain in **L835 ("sounds like meechu")** and **L836 ("sounds like coodju")**. Pattern fix: use the hyphen forms from the L826 teacherScript ("Meet-chu", "Could-joo"). Also check L836-840 for "Aw-lover" (reads as "lover").
- **"VI-ta-min" / "Say the VI part"** (10 hits in world 28: L825 and the reviews): some TTS engines read a bare capital "VI" as the Roman numeral six. I kept it (it matches phonics.sounds "VI" and the slide "vi-ta-min"), but if the voice misreads it, change the spoken form only (for example "VIE-ta-min"), not the table key.
- **All-caps stress words** in rhythm lessons (SEA, SEE, BEST, WANT, ASK): house pattern, kept. If TTS spells any of them out, the fix is in the TTS layer, not the text.
- **List of small words with "and" inside** ("like and, to, for, and can"): fixed in L821/L822. Grep L836-840 for "like and," / "or, and".
- **"I've made this for you"** (L827 core, tested) is a little British; US speakers mostly say "I made this for you". Kept because it is the tested present-perfect item.
- **"Good day"** (L828 phonics word) is rare as a US greeting, but it is a table key used only as a sound example (kept).
- Kept on purpose: all "…" in conversation lines and the L821 title; L821 seg6 short retell; L822 Aino vs Haru saying the same line in script vs conversation; L825 open starter "What do you think is the best?"; L827 seg12 "Then bow and say thank you" (verb frame); L828 "sleeping in it"; L829 "snow all over"; L830 "It's used for playing", "Catch the ball on the cup!", seg8 "Used for tells what it does"; the L822 JA hint （いけん）.

### Run 9 review notes: L831-840 (world 28, Stage 3)

These notes cover school expressions and grammar, vocab, TPR, phonics words and tips, conversation lines and aiTask, recap, teacherScript, and every teacher segment (say, subtitleJa, subtitleEn, slide), plus testPoints. I did not redo or undo the block-wide fixes (OK, fall, soccer, pen pal, US praise, "I say" narration, Hi/Hello openings, plain definitions, "Let's pretend I'm ~" starters). There are 38 patches in 8 lessons; L833 and L837 have no change. There are no learning-item ID changes and no changes to school.expressions[].en, vocab[].en, tpr[].en or phonics words. The Stage 3 wrap-up (journey map, can-do list, friendship speech, rehearsal, festival, letters, question box, Japan introduction, goodbye walk, finale) is warm and well paced, and the rainbow story agrees across lessons (blue and indigo back in L835, "six colors" in L839, violet in L840). The word-parts rules (dis-, un-, -ful, -ness, -ment, y to i, keep or drop e, double n) are all correct. Themes: (1) **Sound spell-outs**: invented respellings "meechu" (L835), "coodju" (L836), "aw-lover" (L839), and "ka-ra-O-ke" (L840), which is the Japanese way and goes against the lesson point (English says kar-ee-OH-kee). All are now hyphen beat forms. (2) **Meaning and story logic**: "Birimo's food was unusual" (L831; it was the village's food), "start school at seven" read as a clock time (L836), "The paper bag for your letter" (L836). (3) **Natural wording**: "I loved is a big, happy like" (L835), "Good day, everyone!" (L838), "an introduction of Japan" (L838), "Writing up!" and bare "Bow!" in speed rounds (L838, L834), "Now you can say what you can do now" (L832), "Ms. Emma gives her some tips" in a script narrated as "I" (L834). (4) **JA**: L839 lines[1] JA said つかう for "eat"; the L831 tip said かさなって for two n's that sit side by side. Every new sentence has 15 words or fewer. The check script passes with ALL OK. All touched lessons are 215-225 s long with 100% English, and every slide item is valid.

#### L831 My Journey So Far
- A: The journey map and the list of past places are natural, and the map reading recaps Stage 3 well. In seg19, "Birimo's food was unusual and delicious" sounds like Birimo cooked it; he said the food at the Village of Springs was unusual. "Look! There is the Tower of Messages, ..." would be "There's" in speech, but it is fine (single flag, kept).
- B: Same flag on seg19. In the seg18 quiz, "not usual, new and surprising" reads as if "not" covers all three words, and unusual does not mean "new". The phonics tipJa says the two n's かさなって (overlap), which suggests they merge into one n.
- C: "Dis and un mean not" reads fine. seg18 also has a choppy list rhythm.
- Decision: seg18 (say, subtitleEn) -> "Which word means not usual, or surprising?" (A+B). seg19 (say, subtitleEn, subtitleJa) -> "Birimo said the food was unusual and delicious." / ビリモは たべものが めずらしくて おいしかったって いってたね。 (A+B). phonics.tipJa かさなって -> ならんで (objective JA).

#### L832 Things I Can Do Now
- A: Kiko's can-do list is a lovely real-classroom reflection. seg18 "Birimo was very A, peaceful, or B, helpful?" is an awkward statement-question. seg20 "Now you can say what you can do now" has "now" twice and sounds clumsy.
- B: Before, I couldn't / Now I can is clear, and the -ful rules (y to i, one l, keep the e in peaceful) are correct. "Before, I was a baby" is fine as Ms. Emma's own example.
- C: Both lines flagged by A are hard to read aloud with a natural rhythm.
- Decision: seg18 (say, subtitleEn, subtitleJa) -> "Was Birimo A, peaceful, or B, helpful?" with JA とても dropped (A+C). seg20 (say, subtitleEn) -> "Now you can tell me what you can do." (A+C).

#### L833 A Speech About Friendship
- A: Riko's speech draft and the Jambo/jam joke are natural and kind. In the script it is Riko's draft that says "Amara taught me Swahili", and in the conversation Kiko says it, but both children may have learned it (kept). "Friendship Festival" (script) vs "friendship festival" (seg0, and the tested L835 core) differ in case (single flag, kept).
- B: "Taught is the past of teach" and the -ness rules (y to i, double n in openness) are correct. "You can make up a name" in seg17 is good for privacy.
- C: Reads well. "Ahem!" is fine.
- Decision: no change.

#### L834 Rehearsal on the Bridge
- A: The speech tips list is real classroom advice. But the teacherScript says "Ms. Emma gives her some tips" and then "Then I say, Look up from your paper": the narrator switches from third person to "I".
- B: Same flag. The block-wide fix changed "Ms. Emma says" to "I say", and this sentence was missed. The -ment rule (keep the e in excitement/amazement, drop it in argument) is correct.
- C: In the seg13 speed round, a bare "Bow!" (twice) is a heteronym, and TTS may read it like the bow on a gift. "Bow to the audience" in seg12 is fine because of the context.
- Decision: teacherScript "Ms. Emma gives her some tips." -> "I give her some tips." (A+B). seg13 (say, subtitleEn) "Bow!" -> "Take a bow!" (B+C).

#### L835 Friendship Festival
- A: The festival program and the claps and piñata are fun. seg8 "I loved is a big, happy like" is not natural English. The seg18 quiz slide shows "festival" and "program", which are not the two choices. "At half past one" is a little British; Americans more often say "one thirty", but it is understood (single flag, kept).
- B: Same flag on seg8. A clear rule is "I loved means I liked it a lot" (JA だいすき だった already matches). The slide should show the choices. seg9 "meechu" is an invented respelling (the script itself uses "Meet-chu").
- C: "a big, happy like" is a stumble. "meechu" may be read oddly.
- Decision: seg8 (say, subtitleEn) -> "I loved means I liked it a lot." (A+B+C). seg9 (say, subtitleEn) "meechu" -> "mee-chu" (B+C). seg18 slide.items -> "Welcome to the friendship festival!" / "I loved Aino's dance. Look! The bridge is shining blue now." (A+B; the bare "I loved Aino's dance." is not valid lesson text).

#### L836 Letters Across the Bridge
- A: The thank-you letters and the privacy line ("we don't write our address") are good. In Riko's letter, "you start school at seven" and the question "When does Aino start school? At seven." read as 7 o'clock, and the next lesson asks "What time does school start". seg4 "The paper bag for your letter" names a different object.
- B: Same flag on "at seven". The fact is about age: Finnish children start school at age seven. "thank you for + ing" and "enjoyed + ing" are explained correctly. seg9 "coodju" is an invented respelling (the script uses "Could-ju").
- C: "coodju" may be misread by TTS.
- Decision: teacherScript "start school at seven" -> "at age seven", and the question -> "When do children in Finland start school? At age seven." (A+B). seg4 (say, subtitleEn) "The paper bag" -> "A paper pocket" (A+B). seg9 (say, subtitleEn) "coodju" -> "could-ju" (B+C). The seg18 quiz slide shows "letter", because the wrong choice "Thank you for share" cannot be a slide item (kept).

#### L837 Questions for the World
- A: The question box card and follow-up questions are natural. "holidays" here means special days (US OK). In the conversation, Haru's question "What time does school start?" gets no answer, but the scene is about questions for the box, and there are already 7 lines (single flag, kept). "Riko asks more." is short but fine (single flag, kept).
- B: The What/When/Where/Why/How tip and "don't ask for names or addresses" are good. The follow-up idea is clear.
- C: Reads well. "Tay-care" is a hyphen form; OK.
- Decision: no change.

#### L838 My Japan Introduction
- A: Riko's draft has nine sentences, and the script count ("There are nine") is correct. seg0 opens with "Good day, everyone!", which is British/Australian and formal, and not in the block-wide opening list. "an introduction of Japan" (teacherScript, scene) should be "an introduction to Japan". The seg13 speed cue "Writing up!" is not natural.
- B: Same flags on "Good day" (JA already こんにちは) and the preposition. The itadakimasu explanation is accurate and kind.
- C: "Writing up!" is an odd cue to say. "may-dov" is a hyphen form; OK.
- Decision: seg0 (say, subtitleEn) "Good day" -> "Hello" (A+B). teacherScript and conversation.scene "introduction of Japan" -> "introduction to Japan" (A+B). seg13 (say, subtitleEn, subtitleJa) "Writing up!" -> "Hold it up!" / もちあげて！ (A+C).

#### L839 Rainbow Bridge Review
- A: The goodbye walk, mochi gift, "Asante" and Iris's card are lovely. The "Tomorrow" in the card is inside the story (kept).
- B: conversation.lines[1] JA "おいわいする ときに つかうんだ" says "use" for "We eat it to celebrate"; seg6 JA already says たべる. seg9 "aw-lover" puts the word "lover" in a kids' sound tip.
- C: "aw-lover" will be read as "a lover". The hyphen beat form "aw-lo-ver" is safer.
- Decision: conversation.lines[1].ja -> おしょうがつを おいわいする ために たべるんだ。 (objective JA). seg9 (say, subtitleEn) "aw-lover" -> "aw-lo-ver" (B+C).

#### L840 World 28 Grand Review
- A: The finale, badge, violet and "Next stop" are a strong ending. seg3 "It feels happy, sad, and kind" is a little loose but child-friendly (single flag, kept).
- B: seg9 "Karaoke, ka-ra-O-ke" is the Japanese syllable split, but the point of the loanword rule is the English way (w28Phonics en キャリオウキ, L816 tip "英語では キャリオウキ").
- C: "ka-ra-O-ke" with a lone capital O reads like Japanese. "kar-ee-OH-kee" is clear.
- Decision: seg9 (say, subtitleEn, subtitleJa) "ka-ra-O-ke" -> "kar-ee-OH-kee" (objective).

#### Block-wide notes
- **Invented respellings from the linking lessons**: L826-829 teach "Meechu. Nice to meechu!", "Could you, coodju. Would you, woodju. Did you, didju.", "All over. Aw-lover." (L821-830 dump). I changed the review copies in L835/L836/L839 to "mee-chu", "could-ju", "aw-lo-ver". Please make the source lessons match (or tell me the form they chose, so the reviews agree). "Aw-lover" is the most important one.
- **karaoke**: L816 seg9 also says "ka-ra-O-ke" while its tipJa says 英語では キャリオウキ. I fixed L840 to "kar-ee-OH-kee"; L816 should use the same form.
- **Capital stress syllables**: "Pi-AN-o", "SU-shi", "HAI-ku", "CAL-en-dar", "JU-do", "MAN-ga" are a house pattern. "AN" and "SU" could be spelled out by some TTS voices. Kept; worth a listen test.
- **Bare word parts in say**: "Dis and un mean not", "Un plus natural", "Ful means full of", "Ness ...", "Ment ..." are read as syllables by most TTS voices. Kept; a listen test would settle it (the fallback is "u, n" / "the ending ful").
- **"Ms. Emma + verb" narration**: L834 "Ms. Emma gives her some tips" was missed by the block-wide "I say" fix. Grep W27-28 teacherScript for `Ms\. Emma (gives|tells|shows|helps|smiles|points)`.
- **Quiz slides**: some quiz slides show vocab, not the choices (L835 fixed; L838 "season", L839 "lighthouse / rice cake", L840 "language / heart" are close enough and kept; L836 cannot show the wrong form).
- Kept on purpose: all "…" lines; "Your friend, Haru" sign-off; "At half past one" (L835); "Friendship Festival" vs "friendship festival" case (L833/L835; the L835 core is tested); L837 unanswered question; L840 "It feels happy, sad, and kind"; story-internal "Tomorrow" in Iris's card (L839); Moi/Jambo/Hola/Asante.

| 786 | `teacherScript` | Hello, everyone! Last time, we said, I hope you feel better soon. Good news! Haru feels much better today. Early in the morning, we hear music in the village square. A young snow monkey is jumping and stretching. His name is Toto. He waves and says, Come and join us! Toto does morning exercises with the village kids. Haru asks him, How often do you exercise? Toto says, Every day! I stretch every morning. Say it with me. How often do you exercise? Super! You can answer, Three times a week. Or, Every day. Let's read the poster in the square. "Morning Exercise in the Village Square! Every day at seven o'clock. Ten minutes of stretching, then ten minutes of walking. Everyone is welcome, young and old. Wear comfortable shoes. | 789 | `teacherScript` | Hello, everyone! Last time, we said, Eating breakfast is good for your brain. Today, it's raining in the village. We stay inside the Spring Inn. Toto comes to visit with his tablet. He loves games and videos. Ms. Emma asks, How much screen time do you have? Haru says, I play video games for one hour a day. Toto says, After one hour, I take a break and go outside. Say it with me. I play video games for one hour a day. Good job! Games and videos are fun. The important thing is a good balance. Let's read a column in the Spring News. "Screen Time Tips. Games and videos can be fun and useful. Here are some tips for a good balance. Talk with your family about a time limit. Rest your eyes every thirty minutes. Look far away for a moment. Turn off screens one hour before bed. Then go outside, play and move your body!" When should you turn off screens? One hour before bed. How often should you rest your eyes? Every thirty minutes. Now, phonics. Talk. Don't say the l. Palm. Don't say the l. Honest. Don't say the h. An honest answer. Calf. | 792 | `teacherScript` | Hello, everyone! Last time, we said, I've lived here for ten years. Granny Capy came to the village ten years ago. Today, it's raining, so we stay inside the Spring Inn. There's an old piano in the lobby. Riko sits down and plays a gentle song. Granny Capy claps her paws. Riko says, I've played the piano since I was five. Say it with me. I've played the piano since I was five. Beautiful! Since tells us the starting point. Riko started at five, and she still plays today. Haru says, I've done karate since I was seven. Kiko loves drawing, and Birimo loves cooking. Granny Capy puts a poster on the lobby wall. Let's read it. "Rainy Day Show at the Spring Inn! This evening, our guests will share their favorite things. Riko will play the piano. Haru will show some karate moves. Kiko will draw pictures of the springs. Birimo will cook sweet potato soup for everyone. Come to the lobby at six o'clock. Everyone is welcome!" What will Kiko do? She will draw pictures of the springs. What time does the show start? At six o'clock. Now, phonics. Since I was five. Since is short. Five is strong. Since April. Since kindergarten. Kin, der, gar, ten. Since Monday. Monday is strong. What have you done for a long time? Tell a friend! | Hello, everyone! | 802 | `teacherScript` | Hello, everyone! Last time, we said, When I'm worried, I talk to my mom. Today, we are sitting by the Heart Spring. The water is warm, and the steam is soft and white. Count to five. Very good! Now breathe（いきを する）with me. Breathe in through your nose. One, two, three, four, five. Breathe out slowly. Kiko smiles and says, I feel calm now. There is a little wooden sign by the spring. Let's read it. "Heart Spring Calm Corner. Sit down on the warm stones and close your eyes. Breathe in through your nose and count to five. Then breathe out slowly through your mouth. Do this three times. Listen to the sound of the water. Feel the warm steam on your face. Now you are ready for a calm and happy day." How many times do you do it? Three times. How do you breathe in? Through your nose. Now, phonics. Piece. A piece of cake. Peace. Quiet and calm. Nose. Touch your nose. Knows. Birimo knows the answer. | 807 | `teacherScript` | Hello, everyone! Last time, we said, On Monday, I'm going to walk to school. Today, we are getting ready for the health quiz. The festival is in two days! Haru rings the bell and answers, Nine to twelve hours. Correct! Then Riko asks, Is it true or false? Kiko shouts, It's true! Dr. Fern wrote the quiz cards. Let's read one card together. "Health Festival Quiz. Question one: How many hours should children sleep? A: five to six hours. B: nine to twelve hours. Question two: True or false? Washing your hands helps stop germs. Question three: True or false? When you feel sick, you should tell an adult. Good luck, everyone!" What is the answer to question one? B, nine to twelve hours. Is question three true or false?
