| 813 | `teacherScript` | Hello, everyone! Last time, we said, In Japan, we take off our shoes at home. Today, we talk about school life on the bridge. Riko says, In Japan, the school year starts in April. How about in your country? Say it with me. How about in your country? Wonderful! Amara says, At my school, the school year starts in January. Mateo says, In Mexico, it starts in late August. Aino says, In Finland, children start school at seven. Kiko is surprised. Seven? Wow! Then Haru says, In Japan, we clean our classroom. Mateo says, That's cool! Let's read Amara's school card. "Jambo! I'm Amara. At my school, the school year starts in January. We wear green uniforms. Our first class starts at eight. My favorite subject is science, because I love the stars. After school, I go running with my friends. How about in your country? Please tell me!" When does Amara's school year start? In January. What is her favorite subject? Science. Now, phonics. These words sound different in English, too. Idea. i-DE-a. Not ai-de-a. Piano. pi-AN-o. Museum. mu-SE-um. Volunteer. vol-un-TEER. The strong part is in the middle or at the end. Let's ask our new friends more questions! | Hello, everyone! Last time, we said, In Japan, we take off our shoes at home. | 816 | `teacherScript` | Hello, everyone! Last time, we said, In both countries, people celebrate New Year. Today, Kiko opens her lunch box. Mateo points and asks, What's that? Kiko says, Onigiri is a kind of rice ball. Say it with me. Onigiri is a kind of rice ball. Wonderful! Kiko says, You can put salmon or umeboshi inside. Aino asks, What's it like? Haru says, It's salty and delicious. Then Birimo eats one, two, three, four onigiri! Birimo, slow down! Let's read Kiko's food cards. "Japanese Food Cards. Onigiri is a kind of rice ball. You can put salmon, tuna, or umeboshi inside. Umeboshi is a sour pickled plum. Miso soup is a kind of soup. It has tofu and green onions in it. Mochi is a kind of rice cake. It is soft and sticky. Enjoy!" What is umeboshi? A sour pickled plum. What is in the miso soup? Tofu and green onions. Now, phonics. English speakers use some Japanese words, too! Sushi. We write s h i for shi. Tofu. We write f u for fu. Tempura. Before p, we write m, not n. Bento. It's a lunch box. Let's explain more Japanese food to our friends! | Hello, everyone! Last time, we said, In both countries, people celebrate New Year. Today, Kiko opens her lunch box. Mateo points and asks, What's that? Kiko says, Onigiri is a kind of rice ball. Say it with me. Onigiri is a kind of rice ball. Wonderful! Kiko says, You can put salmon or umeboshi inside. Aino asks, What's it like? Haru says, It's salty and delicious. Then Birimo eats one, two, three, four onigiri! Birimo, slow down! Let's read Kiko's food cards. "Japanese Food Cards. Onigiri is a kind of rice ball. You can put salmon, tuna, or umeboshi inside. Umeboshi is a sour pickled plum. Miso soup is a kind of soup. It has tofu and green onions in it. Mochi is a kind of rice cake. It is soft and sticky. Enjoy!" What is umeboshi? A sour pickled plum. What is in the miso soup? Tofu and green onions. Now, phonics. English speakers use some Japanese words, too! Sushi. We write s h i for shi. Tofu. We write f u for fu. Tempura. Before p, we write m, not n. Bento. It's a boxed lunch. Let's explain more Japanese food to our friends! | A+B | in US English a lunch box is the container; a bento is the boxed meal |

| 818 | `teacherScript` | Hello, everyone! Last time, we said, A furoshiki is used for wrapping things. Today, our new friends ask, When is the best time to visit Japan? Riko shows a photo of pink trees. You can see cherry blossoms in spring. Say it with me. You can see cherry blossoms in spring. Wonderful! Haru says, In summer, you can watch fireworks. Kiko says, I think autumn is the best. The leaves turn red. Aino says, In Finland, the summer days are very long. Let's read Riko's season guide. "Japan in Four Seasons. In spring, you can see cherry blossoms. People have picnics under the trees. In summer, you can watch fireworks and write wishes for Tanabata. In autumn, the leaves turn red and yellow. In winter, you can see snow festivals in the north. Every season is special!" What can you see in spring? Cherry blossoms. What do people write for Tanabata? Wishes. Now, phonics. These words came from Japan. Haiku. A short Japanese poem. Karaoke. In English, people say ka-ri-O-ki. Tsunami. A very big wave from the sea. We write t s u. Samurai. SA-mu-rai. | 821 | `teacherScript` | Hello, everyone! Last time, we said, Welcome to Japan! Let me show you around. Our video was a big hit on the bridge! Today, we talk about our favorite seasons at Friendship Plaza. Iris asks, Which season is the best? Haru says, I think summer is the best season because we can swim in the sea. Say it with me. I think summer is the best season because we can swim in the sea. Wonderful! First, say what you think. Then, say because and give a reason. Aino says, I think winter is the best because I love snow. In Finland, winter is long and white. Amara asks, Why do you think so? Aino laughs and says, Because skiing is so much fun! Let's read the seasons board on the plaza. "Our Favorite Seasons. Haru likes summer because he can swim in the sea. Aino likes winter because she loves snow and skiing. Amara likes the rainy season because the plants grow fast. Mateo likes autumn because his birthday is in October. Every season is someone's favorite!" Why does Amara like the rainy season? Because the plants grow fast. When is Mateo's birthday? In October. Now, phonics. Listen to the rhythm. I like SUMMER and WINTER. And is small and quick. I want to SWIM in the SEA. To is weak. It's the BEST SEASON for SWIMMING. For is short. We can SEE FIREWORKS in SUMMER. Can is soft. Big words are strong. Small words are weak. Which season do you like best? | Hello, everyone! Last time, we said, Welcome to Japan! I like SUMMER and WINTER. And is small and quick. I want to SWIM in the SEA. To is weak. It's the BEST SEASON for SWIMMING. For is short. We can SEE FIREWORKS in SUMMER. Can is soft. | 829 | `teacherScript` | Hello, everyone! Last time, we said, First, we found a clue. Suddenly, we heard a noise. It must be a bird! Today, we talk about experiences. Do you remember the Cloudtop Railway? We asked, Have you ever seen a rainbow train? Now, Haru asks Mateo, Have you ever made sushi? Say it with me. Have you ever made sushi? Great! Mateo says, No, I haven't. But I've made tacos many times! Then Mateo says, I've played soccer since I was six. In the Village of Springs, we learned since. Do you remember? Amara says, I've never seen snow. Aino smiles and says, You should come to Finland! Let's read the bingo card on the plaza. "Experience Bingo. Find a friend for each box. Have you ever seen snow? Have you ever ridden a train above the clouds? Have you ever made a paper crane? Have you ever been on a stage? Have you ever eaten tacos? Write your friend's name in the box. Get five in a row and shout, Bingo!" How many in a row for bingo? Five. What do you write in the box? Your friend's name. Now, phonics. Don't you. Don-chu. The t and the y join. Take off. Tay-koff. All over. Aw-lover. This summer. Say the s sound only once. Have you ever played bingo in English? | Hello, everyone! Last time, we said, First, we found a clue. Suddenly, we heard a noise. It must be a bird! Today, we talk about experiences. Do you remember the Cloudtop Railway? We asked, Have you ever seen a rainbow train? Now, Haru asks Mateo, Have you ever made sushi? Say it with me. Have you ever made sushi? Great! Mateo says, No, I haven't. But I've made tacos many times! Then Mateo says, I've played soccer since I was six. In the Village of Springs, we learned since. Do you remember? Amara says, I've never seen snow. Aino smiles and says, You should come to Finland! Let's read the bingo card on the plaza. "Experience Bingo. Find a friend for each box. Have you ever seen snow? Have you ever ridden a train above the clouds? Have you ever made a paper crane? Have you ever been on a stage? Have you ever eaten tacos? Write your friend's name in the box. Get five in a row and shout, Bingo!" How many in a row for bingo? Five. What do you write in the box? Your friend's name. Now, phonics. Don't you. Don-chu. The t and the y join. Take off. Tay-koff. All over. Aw-low-ver. This summer. Say the s sound only once. Have you ever played bingo in English? | B+C | 'Aw-lover' reads as 'lover' (wrong vowel); match seg10 |

| 834 | `teacherScript` | Hello, everyone! Last time, we said, Friends can be different, but we can learn from each other. Today is rehearsal day on the bridge. There is a small stage in Friendship Plaza. Kiko goes first. She reads her speech very fast, and she only looks at her paper. Ms. Emma gives her some tips. Speak clearly. Smile at the audience. Say it with me. Speak clearly. Smile at the audience. Great! Then Ms. Emma says, Look up from your paper. Kiko says, OK, I'll try. Remember the theater and the springs? Take a deep breath. Don't hurry. Take your time. Iris hangs a list of speech tips next to the stage. Let's read it. "Five Speech Tips. One, speak clearly and slowly. Two, look up from your paper. Three, smile at the audience. Four, stand up straight. Five, if you make a mistake, don't worry. Just take a breath and go on. Your friends are here to help you. Enjoy your speech!" What is tip number two? Look up from your paper. What do you do after a mistake? Take a breath and go on. Now, phonics. Excitement. Excite plus ment. Keep the e. Argument. Argue, drop the e, plus ment. Enjoyment. Enjoy plus ment. Amazement. Amaze plus ment. Keep the e again. Kiko, you can do it! | Hello, everyone! | 836 | `teacherScript` | Hello, everyone! Last time, we said, Welcome to the friendship festival! The festival was a big success. Now, our new friends are going home soon. So we're writing letters. Haru writes to his pen pal Leo, and Riko writes to Aino. Haru starts his letter like this. Dear friend, thank you for sharing your culture with me. Say it with me. Dear friend, thank you for sharing your culture with me. Great! Then he writes, I enjoyed learning about your school. At the end, he writes, Please write back soon. Your friend, Haru. Remember, we don't write our address or our school name. Let's read Riko's letter to Aino. "Dear Aino, Thank you for sharing your culture with me. I enjoyed learning about your school. I was surprised to hear you start school at seven! Your winter game was so much fun. Someday, I want to see the long summer days in Finland. Could you send me a photo of the snow? Please write back soon. Your friend, Riko" When does Aino start school? At seven. What does Riko ask for? A photo of the snow. Now, phonics. Piano. pi-AN-o. Sushi. A Japanese word in English. Let's ask them to help. Them is small and quick. Could you. Could-ju. Thankfulness. Thank plus ful plus ness. My heart is full of thankfulness! | Hello, everyone! Last time, we said, Welcome to the friendship festival! The festival was a big success. Now, our new friends are going home soon. So we're writing letters. Haru writes to his pen pal Leo, and Riko writes to Aino. Haru starts his letter like this. Dear friend, thank you for sharing your culture with me. Say it with me. Dear friend, thank you for sharing your culture with me. Great! Then he writes, I enjoyed learning about your school. At the end, he writes, Please write back soon. Your friend, Haru. Remember, we don't write our address or our school name. Let's read Riko's letter to Aino. "Dear Aino, Thank you for sharing your culture with me. I enjoyed learning about your school. I was surprised to hear you start school at age seven! Your winter game was so much fun. Someday, I want to see the long summer days in Finland. Could you send me a photo of the snow? Please write back soon. Your friend, Riko" When do children in Finland start school? At age seven. What does Riko ask for? A photo of the snow. Now, phonics. Piano. pi-AN-o. Sushi. A Japanese word in English. Let's ask them to help. Them is small and quick. Could you. Could-ju. Thankfulness. Thank plus ful plus ness. My heart is full of thankfulness! | A+B | 'start school at seven' reads as 7 o'clock (the next lesson asks What time does school start); Finnish children start school at age seven |

| 836 | `teacherScript` | Hello, everyone! Last time, we said, Welcome to the friendship festival! The festival was a big success. Now, our new friends are going home soon. So we're writing letters. Haru writes to his pen pal Leo, and Riko writes to Aino. Haru starts his letter like this. Dear friend, thank you for sharing your culture with me. Say it with me. Dear friend, thank you for sharing your culture with me. Great! Then he writes, I enjoyed learning about your school. At the end, he writes, Please write back soon. Your friend, Haru. Remember, we don't write our address or our school name. Let's read Riko's letter to Aino. "Dear Aino, Thank you for sharing your culture with me. I enjoyed learning about your school. I was surprised to hear you start school at age seven! Your winter game was so much fun. Someday, I want to see the long summer days in Finland. Could you send me a photo of the snow? Please write back soon. Your friend, Riko" When do children in Finland start school? At age seven. What does Riko ask for? A photo of the snow. Now, phonics. Piano. pi-AN-o. Sushi. A Japanese word in English. Let's ask them to help. Them is small and quick. Could you. Could-ju. Thankfulness. Thank plus ful plus ness. My heart is full of thankfulness! | Hello, everyone! | 838 | `teacherScript` | Hello, everyone! Last time, we said, What do children in your country do for fun? Today, we are writing an introduction of Japan for the World Question Box. Riko starts with a big fact. Japan has four seasons. Say it with me. Japan has four seasons. Wonderful! Then she writes about her town. In my town, there is a big festival in summer. A good introduction has a beginning, some facts, and an ending. Let's read Riko's draft. "My Japan. Japan has four seasons. In spring, you can see cherry blossoms. In summer, we watch fireworks. In autumn, the leaves turn red and yellow. In winter, it snows in many places. In my town, there is a big festival in summer. People in Japan say itadakimasu before meals. It means thank you for the food. Please visit Japan someday!" What can you see in spring? Cherry blossoms. What does itadakimasu mean? Thank you for the food. Count the sentences. There are nine. Perfect! Now, phonics. Hamburger. HAM-bur-ger. Judo. A Japanese word in English. We eat lunch at school. At is small and quick. Made of. Made-of, the words hold hands. Unhappiness. Un plus happy, change the y to i, plus ness. Japan is a wonderful place to introduce! | Hello, everyone! Last time, we said, What do children in your country do for fun? Today, we are writing an introduction of Japan for the World Question Box. Riko starts with a big fact. Japan has four seasons. Say it with me. Japan has four seasons. Wonderful!
